hey do you remeber in that code of gnss telemery log we solve how to connect gnss now i am having problem in this code for satellite fix can you solve this error and make it proper "/*
 * test_rf_tx.ino
 * Team 2026 INSPACe CAN 7USAT 025
 *
 * Range test transmitter. Runs on the Teensy 4.1, the CanSat side.
 * Works with test_rf_rx.ino on the ESP32 ground station.
 *
 * This is the sketch to flash on the assembled CanSat for a real range test.
 * Unlike the old bench version it does not send made up numbers. It reads the
 * real barometer, the real inertial unit, the real satellite receiver and the
 * real clock, builds a telemetry packet the same size and shape as the flight
 * packet, transmits it, and writes every reading to the SD card with a real
 * timestamp from the battery backed clock. So after a range test you have both
 * the link numbers from the ground station and a full record on the card.
 *
 * The radio settings are handed to us by the organisers, frequency allocation
 * plan row 025. Do not change the frequency, spreading factor, bandwidth,
 * coding rate, sync word or power.
 *
 * WHAT USES WHICH BUS, so nothing steps on anything else:
 *   radio            SPI1        pins 26 27 39, chip select 29
 *   SD card          SDIO        the Teensy built in card slot
 *   sensors + clock  I2C         pins 18 19
 *   satellites       Serial2     pins 7 8, reset on 6
 *   serial flash     SPI0        pins 10 11 12 13   <-- LEFT ALONE ON PURPOSE
 * The 7Semi serial flash is wired but this sketch never opens SPI0 and never
 * touches pins 10 to 13, so nothing on the flash can be disturbed by a range
 * test. If you want to test the flash use test_flash instead.
 *
 * THE CLOCK IS READ, NEVER WRITTEN. You said the real time is already set and
 * held by the coin cell. This sketch only reads it. Re flashing the Teensy does
 * not touch the clock either, it is a separate chip on its own battery, so you
 * can upload this as many times as you like without losing the time.
 *
 * ANTENNA. Use the RadioMaster dual band whip on the 900 MHz sub gigahertz
 * port, which is ANT2 on the Core1121 XF, not ANT1. It is tuned for this band
 * and has more gain and a cleaner match than the little rubber duck that ships
 * with the module. Keep the whip on both ends pointing the same way, upright,
 * so the two are polarised alike. Full reasoning is printed at the bottom of
 * this comment block in the ground sketch.
 */

#include <Arduino.h>
#include <RadioLib.h>
#include <SPI.h>
#include <Wire.h>
#include <SdFat.h>

/* team id sent on air */
#define TeamId      "025"
#define TeamIdFull  "2026-INSPACe-CAN-7USAT-025"

/* radio settings from the frequency allocation plan, row 025 */
#define FreqMhz       867.00f
#define BwKhz         125.0f
#define Sf            10
#define Cr            5
#define SyncWord      0x98
#define PreambleLen   8
#define TcxoVolts     1.6f
#define UseHpPa       true
#define PowerPad      10
#define PowerFlight   14
#define PacketBytes   99
#define BufMax        160

/* radio pins, the flight wiring */
#define PinCs     29
#define PinIrq    30
#define PinRst    25
#define PinBusy   24
#define PinMosi   26
#define PinSck    27
#define PinMiso   39

/* sensors and clock on I2C */
#define PinSda      18
#define PinScl      19
#define BmpAddr     0x47
#define IcmAddr     0x69
#define RtcAddr     0x68

/* satellite receiver */
#define PinGnssRst  6

/* power sense divider on A6 */
#define PinBusV     A6
#define BusVRatio   (122.0f / 22.0f)

/*
 * Timing for the range test. A 99 byte packet is about 985 ms in the air.
 *
 * The flight firmware runs a full 1 Hz and only opens a listen window every
 * third packet, because at 1 Hz there is no room to both send and listen every
 * second. That makes commands take up to three seconds to land, which is fine
 * in flight but annoying on a range test when you are standing in a field
 * waiting for the calibrate command to take.
 *
 * So this test uses a slightly slower slot, 1500 ms, and listens on EVERY slot.
 * That means once telemetry is running a command lands within about a second
 * and a half every time, not one try in three. It is a deliberate test choice.
 * The graded 1 Hz link is the one in vritra_fc, this is a bench and field tool.
 */
#define TxPeriodMs   1500UL
#define RxWindowMs   450UL
#define TxTimeoutMs  3000UL

/* how often to write a line to the card, once a second */
#define LogPeriodMs  1000UL

/* command numbers, same as the flight computer uses */
#define CmdNone       0
#define CmdTxOn       1
#define CmdTxOff      2
#define CmdCalibrate  3

/* The RF switch table uses pins inside the LR1121 chip, not pins on the
 * Teensy. Copied from Radio.cpp. Do not change these. */
static const uint32_t swPins[] = {
    RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO5, RADIOLIB_LR11X0_DIO6,
    RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC, RADIOLIB_NC
};
static const Module::RfSwitchMode_t swTable[] = {
    { LR11x0::MODE_STBY,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
    { LR11x0::MODE_RX,    { HIGH, LOW  } },
    { LR11x0::MODE_TX,    { HIGH, HIGH } },
    { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HP, { LOW,  HIGH } },
    { LR11x0::MODE_TX_HF, { LOW,  LOW  } },
    { LR11x0::MODE_GNSS,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
    { LR11x0::MODE_WIFI,  { LOW,  LOW  } },
    END_OF_MODE_TABLE,
};

static LR1121 radio = new Module(PinCs, PinIrq, PinRst, PinBusy, SPI1);

/* the radio raises DIO9 both when a packet is sent and when one arrives,
 * so the state below decides which it was */
static volatile bool irqFlag = false;
static void onIrq() { irqFlag = true; }

#define StateIdle    0
#define StateSending 1
#define StateListen  2
#define StateWaiting 3
#define StateFault   4

static uint8_t  state    = StateIdle;
static uint32_t stateAt  = 0;
static uint32_t nextTxMs = 0;
static uint32_t retryAt  = 0;

static bool    txOn       = false;   /* stays off until we are told to start */
static int8_t  txPower    = PowerPad;
static bool    powerDirty = true;

static uint32_t sentCount = 0;
static uint32_t failCount = 0;
static uint32_t cmdCount  = 0;

static float   lastRssi = 0, lastSnr = 0;
static bool    heardGround = false;
static uint8_t lastSeq   = 255;
static uint8_t lastCmdId = CmdNone;   /* echoed in every packet so the ground
                                         can confirm its command landed */

static char     packet[BufMax];
static uint16_t packetLen = 0;
static String   inputLine;

/* ---- sensor state ---- */
static bool     baroOk = false, imuOk = false, rtcOk = false;
static float    padPa  = 101325.0f, padTc = 25.0f;

static uint32_t gnssTime = 0;
static float    gnssLat  = 0.0f, gnssLon = 0.0f, gnssAlt = 0.0f;
static uint8_t  gnssSats = 0;
static bool     gnssFix  = false;
static uint8_t  gnssBuf[1024];
static char     nmea[128];
static uint8_t  nmeaPos = 0;

/* live snapshot, refreshed every read, used by both the packet and the log */
static float    curAlt = 0, curPres = 0, curTemp = 0, curVolt = 0;
static float    curAx = 0, curAy = 0, curAz = 1.0f, curSpin = 0;

/* ---- SD ---- */
static SdFs   sd;
static FsFile logFile;
static bool   cardOk = false;
static char   logName[16] = "RNG000.CSV";
static uint32_t nextLogMs = 0;

/* The card header keeps underscores. That line is graded style data meant to be
 * opened in a spreadsheet, so it must match what a parser expects. Everything a
 * person reads on the serial monitor is written in plain words instead. */
static const char* CsvHeader =
    "TEAM_ID,RTC_TIME,TIME_S,PKT,ALT_M,PRES_PA,TEMP_C,BUS_V,GNSS_TIME,LAT,LON,"
    "GPS_ALT_M,SATS,ACC_X,ACC_Y,ACC_Z,SPIN_RATE,STATE,TX_ON,TX_POWER_DBM,"
    "LAST_CMD,LAST_UP_RSSI";

/* =====================================================================
 * small helpers
 * ===================================================================== */

/* turn a radio error number into words */
static const char* errorText(int16_t code) {
    switch (code) {
    case RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE:                     return "no error";
    case RADIOLIB_ERR_CHIP_NOT_FOUND:           return "chip not found, check the wiring";
    case RADIOLIB_ERR_SPI_CMD_TIMEOUT:          return "the chip never released busy";
    case RADIOLIB_ERR_SPI_CMD_INVALID:          return "the chip rejected that command";
    case RADIOLIB_ERR_SPI_CMD_FAILED:           return "the chip did not report success";
    case RADIOLIB_ERR_INVALID_FREQUENCY:        return "bad frequency";
    case RADIOLIB_ERR_INVALID_OUTPUT_POWER:     return "bad output power";
    case RADIOLIB_ERR_INVALID_BANDWIDTH:        return "bad bandwidth";
    case RADIOLIB_ERR_INVALID_SPREADING_FACTOR: return "bad spreading factor";
    default:                                    return "see the RadioLib error list";
    }
}

static const char* commandName(uint8_t id) {
    switch (id) {
    case CmdTxOn:      return "start telemetry";
    case CmdTxOff:     return "stop telemetry";
    case CmdCalibrate: return "calibrate";
    default:           return "unknown";
    }
}

/* how long a packet of this many bytes stays in the air, in milliseconds */
static float airtime(uint16_t bytes) {
    float symbolMs = ((float)(1UL << Sf) / (BwKhz * 1000.0f)) * 1000.0f;
    float top      = (float)(8 * bytes) - (float)(4 * Sf) + 44.0f;
    float bottom   = (float)(4 * Sf);
    float extra    = ceilf(top / bottom) * (float)Cr;
    if (extra < 0) extra = 0;
    return ((float)PreambleLen + 4.25f + 8.0f + extra) * symbolMs;
}

/* base 36, with a leading minus for negative values so accel and altitude can
 * go below zero. The ground sketch reverses exactly this. */
static void toBase36(char* out, long value) {
    const char* digits = "0123456789ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ";
    char* p = out;
    unsigned long mag;
    if (value < 0) { *p++ = '-'; mag = (unsigned long)(-value); }
    else           {             mag = (unsigned long)value;     }
    char temp[10];
    int  n = 0;
    if (mag == 0) temp[n++] = '0';
    while (mag > 0 && n < 9) { temp[n++] = digits[mag % 36]; mag /= 36; }
    while (n > 0) *p++ = temp[--n];
    *p = '\0';
}

/* =====================================================================
 * I2C and sensors, register sequences lifted from the passing standalone
 * tests test_telemetry_csv and test_barometer so they behave identically
 * ===================================================================== */
static bool wr8(uint8_t dev, uint8_t reg, uint8_t val) {
    Wire.beginTransmission(dev); Wire.write(reg); Wire.write(val);
    return Wire.endTransmission() == 0;
}
static bool rdN(uint8_t dev, uint8_t reg, uint8_t* b, uint8_t n) {
    Wire.beginTransmission(dev); Wire.write(reg);
    if (Wire.endTransmission(false) != 0) return false;
    if (Wire.requestFrom((int)dev, (int)n) != n) return false;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) b[i] = Wire.read();
    return true;
}

static inline int32_t signExtend24(uint32_t x) {
    x &= 0xFFFFFFu;
    return (x & 0x800000u) ? (int32_t)(x | 0xFF000000u) : (int32_t)x;
}

/* --- barometer --- */
static bool baroRead(float& tC, float& pPa) {
    uint8_t b[6];
    if (!rdN(BmpAddr, 0x1D, b, 6)) return false;
    uint32_t tu = ((uint32_t)b[2] << 16) | ((uint32_t)b[1] << 8) | b[0];
    uint32_t pu = ((uint32_t)b[5] << 16) | ((uint32_t)b[4] << 8) | b[3];
    tC  = (float)signExtend24(tu) / 65536.0f;
    pPa = (float)signExtend24(pu) / 64.0f;
    return true;
}
static float aglFromPressure(float pPa) {
    if (pPa <= 0.0f || padPa <= 0.0f) return 0.0f;
    const float t0 = padTc + 273.15f;
    return (t0 / 0.0065f) * (1.0f - powf(pPa / padPa, 0.1902632f));
}
static void baroBegin() {
    uint8_t id = 0;
    baroOk = rdN(BmpAddr, 0x01, &id, 1) && id == 0x51;
    if (!baroOk) return;
    wr8(BmpAddr, 0x7E, 0xB6);                                    delay(10);
    wr8(BmpAddr, 0x37, (0x80 | (0x14 << 2) | 0x00));            delay(5);
    wr8(BmpAddr, 0x31, 0x00);                                    /* IIR off */
    wr8(BmpAddr, 0x36, (0x40 | (0x04 << 3) | 0x01));            /* P x16 T x2 */
    wr8(BmpAddr, 0x37, (0x80 | (0x14 << 2) | 0x01));           delay(30);
    float tC, pPa;
    if (baroRead(tC, pPa)) { padPa = pPa; padTc = tC; }
}

/* --- inertial unit --- */
static bool imuRead(float& ax, float& ay, float& az, float& gz) {
    uint8_t b[12];
    wr8(IcmAddr, 0x7F, 0x00);                /* bank 0 */
    if (!rdN(IcmAddr, 0x2D, b, 12)) return false;
    ax = (int16_t)((b[0] << 8) | b[1])   / 2048.0f;
    ay = (int16_t)((b[2] << 8) | b[3])   / 2048.0f;
    az = (int16_t)((b[4] << 8) | b[5])   / 2048.0f;
    gz = (int16_t)((b[10] << 8) | b[11]) / 16.4f;
    return true;
}
static void imuBegin() {
    wr8(IcmAddr, 0x06, 0x80); delay(100);    /* device reset */
    wr8(IcmAddr, 0x06, 0x01); delay(20);     /* wake, auto clock */
    wr8(IcmAddr, 0x7F, 0x00);
    uint8_t who = 0;
    imuOk = rdN(IcmAddr, 0x00, &who, 1) && who == 0xEA;
    if (!imuOk) return;
    wr8(IcmAddr, 0x7F, 0x20);                /* bank 2 */
    wr8(IcmAddr, 0x14, 0x1F);                /* accel 16 g */
    wr8(IcmAddr, 0x01, 0x2F);                /* gyro 2000 dps */
    wr8(IcmAddr, 0x7F, 0x00);
    delay(30);
}

/* --- clock, READ ONLY, both 12 and 24 hour formats handled without ever
 *     writing a single register --- */
static uint8_t bcd(uint8_t v) { return (uint8_t)((v >> 4) * 10 + (v & 0x0F)); }

static bool rtcReadStamp(char* out, int cap) {
    uint8_t b[7];
    if (!rdN(RtcAddr, 0x00, b, 7)) { snprintf(out, cap, "-"); return false; }
    uint8_t ss = bcd(b[0] & 0x7F);
    uint8_t mm = bcd(b[1] & 0x7F);
    uint8_t hh;
    if (b[2] & 0x40) {                       /* 12 hour mode */
        uint8_t h = bcd(b[2] & 0x1F);
        bool pm = (b[2] & 0x20);
        if (pm && h != 12) h = (uint8_t)(h + 12);
        if (!pm && h == 12) h = 0;
        hh = h;
    } else {
        hh = bcd(b[2] & 0x3F);               /* 24 hour mode */
    }
    uint8_t dd  = bcd(b[4] & 0x3F);
    uint8_t mon = bcd(b[5] & 0x1F);
    uint16_t yr = (uint16_t)(2000 + bcd(b[6]));
    snprintf(out, cap, "%04u-%02u-%02u %02u:%02u:%02u", yr, mon, dd, hh, mm, ss);
    return true;
}

/* --- satellites --- */
static float nmeaToDecimal(const char* s, char hemi) {
    if (!s || *s == '\0') return 0.0f;
    float raw = atof(s);
    int deg = (int)(raw / 100);
    float dec = deg + (raw - deg * 100.0f) / 60.0f;
    if (hemi == 'S' || hemi == 'W') dec = -dec;
    return dec;
}
static void parseNmea(char* s) {
    if (strlen(s) < 9) return;
    char* star = strchr(s, '*');
    if (!star || strlen(star) < 3) return;
    uint8_t want = (uint8_t)strtoul(star + 1, nullptr, 16);
    uint8_t got = 0;
    for (char* p = s + 1; p < star; p++) got ^= (uint8_t)*p;
    if (got != want) return;
    *star = '\0';

    char* f[20];
    int n = 0; f[n++] = s;
    for (char* p = s; *p && n < 20; p++)
        if (*p == ',') { *p = '\0'; f[n++] = p + 1; }
    if (n < 2) return;
    if (strcmp(f[0] + 3, "GGA") != 0) return;
    if (n < 12) return;

    uint8_t q = (uint8_t)atoi(f[6]);
    gnssSats = (uint8_t)atoi(f[7]);
    if (q == 0 || !f[2][0] || !f[4][0]) { gnssFix = false; return; }
    gnssTime = (uint32_t)atof(f[1]);
    gnssLat  = nmeaToDecimal(f[2], f[3][0]);
    gnssLon  = nmeaToDecimal(f[4], f[5][0]);
    gnssAlt  = atof(f[9]);
    gnssFix  = true;
}
static void pollGnss() {
    while (Serial2.available()) {
        char c = (char)Serial2.read();
        if (c == '$') { nmeaPos = 0; nmea[nmeaPos++] = c; continue; }
        if (nmeaPos == 0) continue;
        if (c == '\r' || c == '\n') {
            nmea[nmeaPos] = '\0';
            parseNmea(nmea);
            nmeaPos = 0;
            continue;
        }
        if (nmeaPos < sizeof(nmea) - 1) nmea[nmeaPos++] = c;
        else nmeaPos = 0;
    }
}

/* --- power --- */
static float busVolts() {
    uint32_t acc = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 16; i++) acc += (uint32_t)analogRead(PinBusV);
    return ((float)acc / 16.0f) * (3.3f / 4095.0f) * BusVRatio;
}

/* refresh the whole live snapshot */
static void readSensors() {
    float tC, pPa, ax, ay, az, gz;
    if (baroOk && baroRead(tC, pPa)) {
        curPres = pPa; curTemp = tC; curAlt = aglFromPressure(pPa);
    }
    if (imuOk && imuRead(ax, ay, az, gz)) {
        curAx = ax; curAy = ay; curAz = az; curSpin = fabsf(gz);
    }
    curVolt = busVolts();
    pollGnss();
}

/* =====================================================================
 * SD card
 * ===================================================================== */
static void sdBegin() {
    /* SDIO, the built in slot. This does not open SPI0, so the serial flash on
     * pins 10 to 13 is never touched. */
    if (!sd.begin(SdioConfig(FIFO_SDIO))) {
        Serial.println("No SD card found, the range test will still run but");
        Serial.println("nothing will be recorded. Check the card and reset.");
        cardOk = false;
        return;
    }
    /* pick the next free file so a reset never writes over the last run */
    for (uint16_t i = 0; i < 1000; i++) {
        snprintf(logName, sizeof(logName), "RNG%03u.CSV", i);
        if (!sd.exists(logName)) break;
    }
    if (!logFile.open(logName, O_WRITE | O_CREAT | O_TRUNC)) {
        Serial.println("Could not create the log file on the card");
        cardOk = false;
        return;
    }
    logFile.println(CsvHeader);
    logFile.sync();
    cardOk = true;
    Serial.print("Recording to ");
    Serial.print(logName);
    Serial.println(" on the card");
}

static void logRow() {
    if (!cardOk) return;
    char stamp[24];
    rtcReadStamp(stamp, sizeof(stamp));

    char row[240];
    int n = snprintf(row, sizeof(row),
        "%s,%s,%lu,%lu,%.1f,%.0f,%.1f,%.2f,",
        TeamIdFull, stamp,
        (unsigned long)(millis() / 1000UL), (unsigned long)sentCount,
        curAlt, curPres, curTemp, curVolt);

    if (gnssFix)
        n += snprintf(row + n, sizeof(row) - n, "%06lu,%.7f,%.7f,%.1f,%u,",
                      (unsigned long)gnssTime, gnssLat, gnssLon, gnssAlt, gnssSats);
    else
        n += snprintf(row + n, sizeof(row) - n, "-,-,-,-,%u,", gnssSats);

    n += snprintf(row + n, sizeof(row) - n, "%.2f,%.2f,%.2f,%.1f,%d,%d,%d,%u,%.1f",
                  curAx * 9.80665f, curAy * 9.80665f, curAz * 9.80665f, curSpin,
                  txOn ? 3 : 0,               /* state, 3 is a stand in for on air */
                  txOn ? 1 : 0, (int)txPower, lastCmdId,
                  heardGround ? lastRssi : 0.0f);

    logFile.println(row);
    static uint8_t sinceSync = 0;
    if (++sinceSync >= 5) { sinceSync = 0; logFile.sync(); }
}

/* =====================================================================
 * radio
 * ===================================================================== */
static void showHelp() {
    Serial.println();
    Serial.println("Type one of these:");
    Serial.println("  ON             start sending telemetry");
    Serial.println("  OFF            stop sending");
    Serial.println("  POWER PAD      use 10 dBm, the on pad level");
    Serial.println("  POWER FLIGHT   use 14 dBm, the in flight level");
    Serial.println("  CAL            recapture the ground pressure reference");
    Serial.println("  STATUS         show the totals");
    Serial.println("  HELP           show this list");
    Serial.println();
    Serial.println("You can also drive it from the ground station over the air.");
    Serial.println();
}

static void showStatus() {
    Serial.println();
    Serial.print("Sending is ");
    Serial.println(txOn ? "on" : "off");
    Serial.print("Power ");
    Serial.print(txPower);
    Serial.println(" dBm");
    Serial.print("Packets sent ");
    Serial.println(sentCount);
    Serial.print("Failed ");
    Serial.println(failCount);
    Serial.print("Commands received ");
    Serial.println(cmdCount);
    Serial.print("Last command ");
    Serial.println(commandName(lastCmdId));
    Serial.print("Barometer ");
    Serial.println(baroOk ? "ready" : "not responding");
    Serial.print("Inertial unit ");
    Serial.println(imuOk ? "ready" : "not responding");
    Serial.print("Clock ");
    if (rtcOk) { char s[24]; rtcReadStamp(s, sizeof(s)); Serial.println(s); }
    else       Serial.println("not responding");
    Serial.print("Satellite fix ");
    if (gnssFix) { Serial.print("yes, "); Serial.print(gnssSats); Serial.println(" sats"); }
    else         { Serial.print("no, "); Serial.print(gnssSats); Serial.println(" visible"); }
    Serial.print("Card ");
    Serial.println(cardOk ? logName : "not recording");
    if (heardGround) {
        Serial.print("Last uplink RSSI ");
        Serial.print(lastRssi, 1);
        Serial.print(" dBm, SNR ");
        Serial.print(lastSnr, 1);
        Serial.println(" dB");
    } else {
        Serial.println("The ground station has not been heard yet");
    }
    Serial.println();
}

static bool startRadio() {
    /* These three lines must come before SPI1.begin(). The Teensy uses a
     * different pin for SPI1 receive by default and our board is wired to 39.
     * Getting this wrong gives error 707, which looks like a dead chip. */
    SPI1.setMISO(PinMiso);
    SPI1.setMOSI(PinMosi);
    SPI1.setSCK(PinSck);
    SPI1.begin();

    radio.setRfSwitchTable(swPins, swTable);
    int16_t st = radio.begin(FreqMhz, BwKhz, Sf, Cr, SyncWord,
                             txPower, PreambleLen, TcxoVolts);
    if (st != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
        Serial.print("The radio did not start. ");
        Serial.println(errorText(st));
        return false;
    }
    radio.setRfSwitchTable(swPins, swTable);   /* begin() wipes it */
    radio.setCRC(2);
    radio.setRegulatorDCDC();
    radio.setSyncWord(SyncWord);
    if (radio.setOutputPower(txPower, UseHpPa) == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE)
        powerDirty = false;
    if (radio.calibrateImageRejection(863.0f, 870.0f) != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE)
        Serial.println("Image calibration did not finish cleanly");
    if (radio.setRxBoostedGainMode(true) != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE)
        Serial.println("Could not turn on boosted receive gain");

    Serial.print("Radio ready on ");
    Serial.print(FreqMhz, 2);
    Serial.print(" MHz, SF");
    Serial.print(Sf);
    Serial.print(", ");
    Serial.print(BwKhz, 0);
    Serial.print(" kHz, CR 4 of ");
    Serial.print(Cr);
    Serial.print(", ");
    Serial.print(txPower);
    Serial.println(" dBm");

    radio.setIrqAction(onIrq);
    irqFlag = false;
    return true;
}

/* write the power to the chip only when the radio is free, otherwise the write
 * is lost and the chip stays at the old level while we think it changed */
static void applyPower() {
    if (!powerDirty) return;
    if (state != StateIdle && state != StateWaiting) return;
    if (radio.setOutputPower(txPower, UseHpPa) == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
        powerDirty = false;
        Serial.print("Power is now ");
        Serial.print(txPower);
        Serial.println(" dBm");
    }
}

static void listenForever() {
    irqFlag = false;
    if (radio.startReceive() == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) state = StateWaiting;
    else { state = StateFault; retryAt = millis() + 1000; }
    stateAt = millis();
}

/*
 * Build the packet from the live snapshot. Same twenty fields and same order as
 * the flight telemetry packet, so the airtime matches. Two extra fields ride at
 * the end, the transmit power and the id of the last command we obeyed, so the
 * ground station can see its command was received. Then it is padded to 99
 * bytes so the air time is representative of the real link.
 */
static uint16_t makePacket(char* out) {
    char t[10], p[10], alt[12], pres[10], temp[10], volt[10], gt[10];
    char lat[12], lon[12], galt[10], sats[10];
    char ax[10], ay[10], az[10], spin[10], st[10], flags[10], pwr[10], lc[10];

    toBase36(t,     (long)(millis() / 1000UL));
    toBase36(p,     (long)(sentCount + 1));
    toBase36(alt,   (long)lroundf(curAlt * 10.0f));
    toBase36(pres,  (long)lroundf(curPres));
    toBase36(temp,  (long)lroundf(curTemp * 10.0f));
    toBase36(volt,  (long)lroundf(curVolt * 100.0f));
    toBase36(gt,    (long)gnssTime);
    toBase36(lat,   (long)lroundf(gnssLat * 100000.0f));
    toBase36(lon,   (long)lroundf(gnssLon * 100000.0f));
    toBase36(galt,  (long)lroundf(gnssAlt * 10.0f));
    toBase36(sats,  (long)gnssSats);
    toBase36(ax,    (long)lroundf(curAx * 1000.0f));
    toBase36(ay,    (long)lroundf(curAy * 1000.0f));
    toBase36(az,    (long)lroundf(curAz * 1000.0f));
    toBase36(spin,  (long)lroundf(curSpin * 10.0f));
    toBase36(st,    (long)(txOn ? 3 : 0));
    toBase36(flags, (long)(imuOk ? 0x40 : 0) | (baroOk ? 0x20 : 0) | (gnssFix ? 1 : 0));
    toBase36(pwr,   (long)txPower);
    toBase36(lc,    (long)lastCmdId);

    int n = snprintf(out, BufMax,
        "%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,F0,%s,%s",
        TeamId, t, p, alt, pres, temp, volt, gt, lat, lon, galt, sats,
        ax, ay, az, spin, st, flags, pwr, lc);
    if (n <= 0 || n >= BufMax) return 0;

    /* Pad the optional part so the packet reaches the real size. The padding
     * goes after a comma so it is its own field. Without the comma the X
     * letters would join onto the last value, and X is a real base 36 digit,
     * so the ground station would read that value wrong. */
    if (n < PacketBytes - 3 && n < BufMax - 4) {
        out[n++] = ',';
        while (n < PacketBytes - 2 && n < BufMax - 3) out[n++] = 'X';
    }
    out[n++] = '\r';
    out[n++] = '\n';
    out[n]   = '\0';
    return (uint16_t)n;
}

static void readUplink() {
    char buf[64];
    int  len = radio.getPacketLength();
    if (len <= 0 || len >= (int)sizeof(buf)) { radio.startReceive(); return; }
    if (radio.readData((uint8_t*)buf, (size_t)len) != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
        radio.startReceive();
        return;
    }
    buf[len] = '\0';
    lastRssi = radio.getRSSI();
    lastSnr  = radio.getSNR();
    heardGround = true;

    while (len > 0 && (buf[len-1] == '\r' || buf[len-1] == '\n')) buf[--len] = '\0';

    /* the message looks like  025,C,<id>,<arg>,<seq>  */
    int idLen = strlen(TeamId);
    bool mine = (len > idLen + 4 && strncmp(buf, TeamId, idLen) == 0 &&
                 buf[idLen] == ',' && buf[idLen+1] == 'C');

    Serial.println();
    Serial.println("Heard the ground station");
    Serial.print("  Message  ");
    Serial.println(buf);
    Serial.print("  RSSI     ");
    Serial.print(lastRssi, 1);
    Serial.println(" dBm");
    Serial.print("  SNR      ");
    Serial.print(lastSnr, 1);
    Serial.println(" dB");

    if (mine) {
        int id = 0, arg = 0, seq = -1;
        if (sscanf(buf + idLen + 3, "%d,%d,%d", &id, &arg, &seq) == 3 &&
            (uint8_t)seq != lastSeq) {
            lastSeq   = (uint8_t)seq;
            lastCmdId = (uint8_t)id;         /* echoed back so the ground knows */
            cmdCount++;
            if (id == CmdTxOn) {
                txOn = true;
                Serial.println("  Command  start telemetry");
            } else if (id == CmdTxOff) {
                txOn = false;
                Serial.println("  Command  stop telemetry");
            } else if (id == CmdCalibrate) {
                float tC, pPa;
                if (baroOk && baroRead(tC, pPa)) { padPa = pPa; padTc = tC; }
                Serial.println("  Command  calibrate, ground pressure recaptured");
            } else {
                Serial.print("  Command  number ");
                Serial.println(id);
            }
        }
    } else {
        Serial.println("  Not addressed to us, ignored");
    }
    Serial.println();
    radio.startReceive();
}

static void runRadio() {
    uint32_t now = millis();

    if (state == StateFault) {
        if ((int32_t)(now - retryAt) >= 0) {
            if (startRadio()) { state = StateIdle; nextTxMs = now; stateAt = now; }
            else retryAt = now + 1000;
        }
        return;
    }

    if (state == StateIdle) {
        applyPower();
        if (!txOn) { listenForever(); return; }   /* silent, so listen always */
        if ((int32_t)(now - nextTxMs) < 0) return;

        readSensors();                            /* fresh values for this packet */
        packetLen = makePacket(packet);
        if (packetLen == 0) return;
        irqFlag = false;
        if (radio.startTransmit((uint8_t*)packet, packetLen) != RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE) {
            failCount++;
            state = StateFault;
            retryAt = now + 500;
            return;
        }
        state    = StateSending;
        stateAt  = now;
        nextTxMs = now + TxPeriodMs;
        return;
    }

    if (state == StateSending) {
        if (irqFlag) {
            irqFlag = false;
            radio.finishTransmit();
            sentCount++;

            char shown[BufMax];
            strncpy(shown, packet, BufMax - 1);
            shown[BufMax - 1] = '\0';
            int k = strlen(shown);
            while (k > 0 && (shown[k-1] == '\r' || shown[k-1] == '\n')) shown[--k] = '\0';

            Serial.println();
            Serial.print("Packet ");
            Serial.println(sentCount);
            Serial.print("  Content  ");
            Serial.println(shown);
            Serial.print("  Bytes    ");
            Serial.println(packetLen);
            Serial.print("  Height   ");
            Serial.print(curAlt, 1);
            Serial.println(" m above the ground reference");
            Serial.print("  Sats     ");
            Serial.println(gnssSats);
            Serial.print("  Power    ");
            Serial.print(txPower);
            Serial.println(" dBm");

            /* listen on EVERY slot, that is the whole point of the test cadence */
            state = (radio.startReceive() == RADIOLIB_ERR_NONE)
                  ? StateListen : StateIdle;
            stateAt = now;
        } else if (now - stateAt > TxTimeoutMs) {
            failCount++;
            state = StateFault;
            retryAt = now + 200;
        }
        return;
    }

    if (state == StateListen) {
        if (irqFlag) {
            irqFlag = false;
            readUplink();
            radio.standby();
            state = StateIdle;
            stateAt = now;
        } else if (now - stateAt > RxWindowMs) {
            radio.standby();
            state = StateIdle;
            stateAt = now;
        }
        return;
    }

    if (state == StateWaiting) {
        applyPower();
        if (txOn) {
            radio.standby();
            state = StateIdle;
            nextTxMs = now;
            stateAt = now;
            return;
        }
        if (irqFlag) { irqFlag = false; readUplink(); stateAt = now; }
        else if (now - stateAt > 10000UL) listenForever();
        return;
    }
}

static void readSerial() {
    while (Serial.available()) {
        char c = (char)Serial.read();
        if (c != '\r' && c != '\n') {
            if (inputLine.length() < 32) inputLine += c;
            continue;
        }
        if (inputLine.length() == 0) continue;
        String cmd = inputLine;
        inputLine = "";
        cmd.trim();
        cmd.toUpperCase();

        if (cmd == "ON") {
            txOn = true;  lastCmdId = CmdTxOn;
            Serial.println("Sending started");
        } else if (cmd == "OFF") {
            txOn = false; lastCmdId = CmdTxOff;
            Serial.println("Sending stopped");
        } else if (cmd == "POWER PAD") {
            txPower = PowerPad;    powerDirty = true;
            Serial.println("Switching to pad power");
        } else if (cmd == "POWER FLIGHT") {
            txPower = PowerFlight; powerDirty = true;
            Serial.println("Switching to flight power");
        } else if (cmd == "CAL" || cmd == "CALIBRATE") {
            float tC, pPa;
            if (baroOk && baroRead(tC, pPa)) {
                padPa = pPa; padTc = tC;
                Serial.print("Ground pressure recaptured, ");
                Serial.print(padPa, 1);
                Serial.println(" Pa, height now reads zero here");
            } else {
                Serial.println("Barometer not responding, nothing changed");
            }
        } else if (cmd == "STATUS") {
            showStatus();
        } else if (cmd == "HELP") {
            showHelp();
        } else {
            Serial.println("I did not understand that. Type HELP for the list.");
        }
    }
}

void setup() {
    Serial.begin(115200);
    uint32_t t0 = millis();
    while (!Serial && millis() - t0 < 3000) { }

    Serial.println();
    Serial.println("Range test transmitter, team 2026 INSPACe CAN 7USAT 025");
    Serial.println("This board stays silent until it is told to start,");
    Serial.println("either by typing ON here or by a command from the ground.");

    /* 100 kHz, not 400 kHz. Measured on this harness, 400 kHz makes the
     * inertial unit answer its address and then return all ones on every read. */
    Wire.begin();
    Wire.setClock(100000);
    analogReadResolution(12);

    /* satellites, a clean reset pulse then open the port with a big buffer so
     * a long transmit slot does not overrun it and lose the fix */
    pinMode(PinGnssRst, OUTPUT);
    digitalWrite(PinGnssRst, LOW);
    delay(60);
    digitalWrite(PinGnssRst, HIGH);
    Serial2.addMemoryForRead(gnssBuf, sizeof(gnssBuf));
    Serial2.begin(9600);

    baroBegin();
    imuBegin();

    /* clock, read only. If the hour comes back in 12 hour format it is decoded
     * in software, nothing is ever written, so the time you set stays exactly
     * as it is. */
    { char s[24]; rtcOk = rtcReadStamp(s, sizeof(s)); }

    Serial.print("Barometer      "); Serial.println(baroOk ? "ready" : "not responding");
    Serial.print("Inertial unit  "); Serial.println(imuOk  ? "ready" : "not responding");
    if (rtcOk) { char s[24]; rtcReadStamp(s, sizeof(s));
                 Serial.print("Clock          "); Serial.println(s); }
    else       Serial.println("Clock          not responding");
    Serial.print("Pad pressure   "); Serial.print(padPa, 1); Serial.println(" Pa");

    sdBegin();

    while (!startRadio()) {
        Serial.println("Trying the radio again in two seconds");
        delay(2000);
    }

    Serial.print("Packet size ");
    Serial.print(PacketBytes);
    Serial.print(" bytes, airtime ");
    Serial.print(airtime(PacketBytes), 0);
    Serial.print(" ms, slot ");
    Serial.print(TxPeriodMs);
    Serial.println(" ms with a listen window every slot");

    showHelp();

    state     = StateIdle;
    stateAt   = millis();
    nextTxMs  = millis();
    nextLogMs = millis();
}

void loop() {
    readSerial();
    pollGnss();
    runRadio();

    /* record to the card once a second, whatever the radio is doing, so the
     * whole session is on the card with a real timestamp */
    uint32_t now = millis();
    if (cardOk && (int32_t)(now - nextLogMs) >= 0) {
        nextLogMs = now + LogPeriodMs;
        if (state != StateSending) readSensors();   /* fresh unless mid packet */
        logRow();
    }
}
"

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T06:53:04)

Yes — I remember. The GNSS fix in the earlier `test_telemetry_csv.ino` session came down to draining `Serial2` fast enough that the checksum never gets cut mid-sentence, and the fix for that is `addMemoryForRead()` on a big buffer. That's in this file too — but the two lines are in the wrong order:

```cpp
Serial2.addMemoryForRead(gnssBuf, sizeof(gnssBuf));
Serial2.begin(9600);
```

`begin()` resets the UART's internal buffer state, and the proven-working order (both in `Sensors.cpp` and in PJRC's own Teensyduino example) is `begin()` **first**, then `addMemoryForRead()` after — not before. Called in this order, the 1024-byte buffer is silently discarded and the port falls back to Teensy's default 64-byte ring buffer, which is exactly the trap already memorialized: at 9600 baud that's only 66 ms of headroom, one GGA/RMC/GSA/GSV burst is 400–700 ms of data, and any loop stall (SD `sync()`, waiting on radio IRQ) overruns it. Bytes vanish mid-sentence, the checksum in `parseNmea()` fails, the line gets silently discarded, and `gnssFix` never goes true — which reads exactly like "no satellite fix" even with a perfectly healthy receiver outside with a clear sky view.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T06:53:16)

Fixed — that's the one deviation from the already-solved pattern. Everything else in the GNSS path already matches what was verified working: baud 9600, RST pin driven LOW then released HIGH before `begin()`, byte-at-a-time parsing from `$`, checksum validated before trusting any field, and the fix gated on both quality > 0 **and** a non-empty lat field (so a 1-satellite partial fix with empty lat/lon doesn't get reported as 0.0000000).

**No toolchain here, so this is written and unverified** — I haven't compiled it, only traced it against the known-good `Sensors.cpp` order. Reflash and check: outdoors, clear sky, give it a minute or two for first fix, and `STATUS` should start showing sats climbing and then `Satellite fix yes`.

I've also saved this to memory so the ordering trap surfaces automatically next time GNSS bring-up comes up in this project, instead of needing you to remember to ask.

Listens for the CanSat and sends it commands.
Radio ready on 867.00 MHz, SF10, 125 kHz, CR 4 of 5
Listening now

Type one of these, then Enter:
  TX ON        tell the CanSat to start sending telemetry
  TX OFF       tell the CanSat to stop sending
  CALIBRATE    zero the barometer at the current spot
  STATUS       show the totals so far
  HELP         show this list

Range test receiver, team 2026 INSPACe CAN 7USAT 025
Listens for the CanSat and sends it commands.
Radio ready on 867.00 MHz, SF10, 125 kHz, CR 4 of 5
Listening now

Type one of these, then Enter:
  TX ON        tell the CanSat to start sending telemetry
  TX OFF       tell the CanSat to stop sending
  CALIBRATE    zero the barometer at the current spot
  STATUS       show the totals so far
  HELP         show this list

If a command has no effect, the CanSat may be out of range.
Watch for the line that says the CanSat confirmed it.

Sending start telemetry to the CanSat, will keep trying until it confirms
No confirmation yet for start telemetry. Send it again if the CanSat still has not reacted.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:09:28)

The ordering fix was real and worth keeping, but 496 packets — many minutes — with `Sats 0` the entire time is far longer than a normal GNSS time-to-first-fix outdoors. There's a stronger clue in your own data: `Height` sits under 2 m the whole run and the RSSI trace looks exactly like the earlier wall/indoor test (−80 dBm sliding down to −110 dBm). If this run is indoors, that alone explains it — GNSS and LoRa are not comparable here. A LoRa signal at 867 MHz punches through a wall with tens of dB to spare because the transmitter is a few metres away. A GPS satellite is 20,000 km away transmitting at a few watts; the signal arriving at your antenna is already around −130 dBm in the open, with almost no margin left. A roof or a single wall is often enough to take it below the noise floor entirely — LoRa surviving a wall tells you nothing about whether GNSS can.

The sketch currently can't tell you which situation you're in, because it only prints the *decoded* result, never whether the module is talking at all. That's the actual gap — so I'm adding a real diagnostic instead of guessing further.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:09:45)

Now I'll wire in the counters at every branch point of `parseNmea`, so each distinct failure mode is distinguishable:

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:09:56)

Now surface those counters in `showStatus()` with a plain-language verdict, so the answer is legible without cross-referencing anything:

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:09:59)

Let me find `showStatus()`.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:10:18)

Also worth adding the same three counters as CSV-friendly columns to the packet stream so this diagnosis works over the air too, not just on the CanSat's own local serial monitor — that's the whole reason to be running a field test in the first place.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:10:54)

I stopped short of adding these to the over-the-air packet — your ground sketch (`test_rf_rx.ino`) already decodes specific fields like Height and Sats, and you've since customized it further yourself (the "Range test receiver and commander" version). Extending the wire layout without seeing your current RX decode logic risks exactly the kind of silent field-misalignment bug this whole GNSS story has already been about, so I kept the new diagnostics local to the Teensy's own serial monitor.

**What to do next:** plug a USB cable straight into the CanSat's Teensy (not the ESP32 ground station), open its serial monitor, take it outside with a clear view of the sky, and type `STATUS`. Three new lines will tell you exactly which of these is happening:

- **`bytes seen: 0`** → the module isn't talking at all. Check the wiring on pins 6, 7, 8 — this is a real fault, not the sky.
- **`bytes seen: nonzero, sentences read cleanly: 0`** → data is arriving but nothing parses. Points at baud rate or the reset pulse — also a real fault.
- **`sentences read cleanly: nonzero, no fix`** → the receiver is healthy and every sentence checks out, it's simply waiting on satellites. It will also print the last raw sentence it heard, so you can see real satellite count and fix-quality fields with your own eyes. This case is the sky, not the code — give it a couple of minutes outdoors, clear of the aluminium plates and any roof.

Given your pasted log — `Height` sitting under 2 m the whole run and RSSI sliding from −80 to −110 dBm, matching your earlier documented indoor/wall test — my working guess is you're indoors right now, and that alone accounts for zero sats regardless of the code. But the `STATUS` output will say which of the three it actually is instead of leaving it a guess.

I've saved this — GNSS bring-up plus the earlier SPI ordering fix — to `[[cansat-sensor-bringup-traps]]` so it doesn't need re-deriving next time.

---

2. Key Technical Concepts:
- LR1121 / Core1121-XF radio module: HP PA path only wired (R3=0R, R4=NC), ~30 dB loss if LP PA used or wrong antenna port
- Two separate antenna pads: LORA_ANT (ANT2, sub-GHz) and 2.4G_ANT (ANT1); must use LORA_ANT for 867 MHz
- VSWR to mismatch loss: a well-matched antenna (VSWR 1.25) cannot produce 30 dB of mismatch loss; even VSWR 10:1 is only 4.8 dB
- Link margin = received power (dBm) − receiver sensitivity (dBm)
- SF10/BW125 sensitivity derivation: S = −174 + 10·log10(125000) + NF(5.5) + SNRmin(−15) ≈ −132 dBm
- Free-space path loss (20 dB/decade): applies in flight and within two-ray breakpoint
- Two-ray ground reflection (40 dB/decade beyond breakpoint): applies to ground testing and post-landing. Breakpoint = 4π·h_t·h_r/λ (NOT 4·h_t·h_r/λ — the earlier calculation was wrong by this factor)
- GNSS (AG3335M / Quectel L89): Serial2 at 9600 baud, RST pin 6 active LOW/idle HIGH, `Serial2.begin(9600)` MUST come BEFORE `Serial2.addMemoryForRead()` — `begin()` resets buffer state, so calling addMemoryForRead first silently discards the big buffer and leaves the 64-byte default, starving the receiver
- BMP585: registers 0x1D (TEMP_XLSB, 6-byte burst), init needs STANDBY step before OSR write and IIR off at 0x31
- ICM-20948 at I2C address 0x69 (AD0 high); DS3231 RTC at 0x68 — never send device reset to 0x68 (that's the RTC YEAR register)
- Teensy SPI1: setMISO(39) / setMOSI(26) / setSCK(27) MUST precede SPI1.begin()
- setRfSwitchTable must be called TWICE: before radio.begin() and again after (begin() clears it)
- setOutputPower(N, true) — the second argument (true = HP PA) is mandatory on the Core1121-XF
- LoRa time on air (Semtech AN1200.13): at SF10/BW125, 99-byte packet ≈ 985 ms in a 1000 ms slot — explains why listen window can only open every 3rd cycle at true 1 Hz flight cadence
- Base36 codec: MUST NOT be copied into cdr_tests — kept isolated in vritra_fc/Base36.h to prevent drift; test sketches use their own simple toBase36/fromBase36 scaffolding

A 30 dB gap with confirmed-correct port and connector is a fault signature (Radio.cpp load-bearing fact #1), not an antenna quality difference.

- **Wrong two-ray breakpoint formula**: Gave ground-test range table using `d_b = 4·h_t·h_r/λ` and stacked free-space on top of two-ray at the breakpoint — double-counted, table was ~10 dB pessimistic. Corrected: breakpoint is `4π·h_t·h_r/λ` and the rule is "take whichever model predicts more loss, not both."

- **Padding corruption bug**: The XX padding to bring packet to 99 bytes was appended directly after the last base-36 value field with no separator. X is a valid base-36 digit so the receiver would decode a garbage power value. Fixed by inserting a comma before the padding so it becomes its own field.

- **GNSS buffer ordering**: `Serial2.addMemoryForRead()` was called BEFORE `Serial2.begin()`. `begin()` resets UART internal state, silently discarding the big buffer. Fixed by swapping order: `Serial2.begin(9600)` first, then `Serial2.addMemoryForRead(gnssBuf, 1024)`.

- **Serial output underscores**: Earlier versions of test sketches used underscores in human-readable output. User corrected: "dont add '_' and '-' in code in serial monitor." All human-readable Serial.print output now uses plain words. Underscores kept only in CSV header strings.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:21:01)

Good, I have everything needed. Here's the full picture:

**GNSS (L89H / AG3335M) — commands are correct**

The flight firmware sends three config commands on boot (`GNSS_SEND_CONFIG=true`):

| Command | What it does | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `$PAIR066,1,1,1,0,0,1` | GPS + GLONASS + Galileo + NavIC (6th param) | **Correct** — this is the PAIR-native Airoha command |
| `$PMTK353,1,1,1,0,0` | GPS + GLONASS + Galileo, BDS off | MTK fallback only, **no NavIC param** — deliberately kept as a safety net if a non-PAIR module variant ever shows up |
| `$PMTK869,1,1` | EASY — orbit predictions saved to internal flash, faster subsequent fixes | Correct |

Then `DataLogger.cpp` adds hot-start aids after the first GPS fix: `$PMTK740` (time) and `$PMTK741` (position + time), and re-asserts `$PMTK869`.

One real-world caveat: NavIC civil signal is **L5 (1176 MHz)**, not L1. If your patch antenna is L1-only (which it probably is unless you bought an L1+L5 dual-band part), NavIC satellites will stay at zero counts no matter what — the command ACKs fine but the hardware simply cannot receive L5. That's not a firmware bug.

**7Semi — "7Semi" here is library names, not a chip command set**

The `README.md` line is just recording that the old third-party `7Semi_BMP585` and `7Semi_ICM20948` Arduino libraries were removed and replaced with direct register-level code in `Sensors.cpp`. There's no "7Semi startup command sequence" — BMP585 and ICM-20948 are initialized by writing to their own I2C registers (OSR, ODR, DEVICE_RESET, etc.), not by any 7Semi-specific protocol. If you're seeing something labelled "7Semi" in a startup error, it's likely that old library trying to initialise and conflicting.

So: GNSS commands are correct as written. The 7Semi mention is just library attribution.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:28:13)

Found it. There are two differences between vritra_fc and test_rf_tx.ino that together explain the 0 sats:

**Problem 1 — wrong serial port detection.** `vritra_fc` listens on BOTH Serial2 (pins 7/8, rev 2026-08 harness) AND Serial1 (pins 0/1, older harness) for up to 3 seconds, then locks to whichever speaks first. `test_rf_tx.ino` only opens Serial2. If your physical GNSS wire is still on the old Serial1 pins, the test sketch never sees a single byte.

**Problem 2 — no config commands sent.** vritra_fc sends `PAIR066,1,1,1,0,0,1` (constellation select), `PMTK353,1,1,1,0,0` (MTK fallback), and `PMTK869,1,1` (EASY orbit cache), with a 300 ms settle wait after reset before the first command. The test sketch sends nothing.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:29:25)

Done. Here's exactly what changed and why each part matters:

---

**What was added / changed:**

1. **`gnssAutodetect()`** (new function, replaces the old Serial2-only open block in `setup()`):
   - Opens both Serial2 (pins 7/8) and Serial1 (pins 0/1) with 1024-byte buffers each
   - Waits up to 3 seconds for either to send a `$` character
   - Locks `gnssPort` to whichever one spoke, prints which port it chose, then closes the other
   - After lock, waits 300 ms then sends `PAIR066`, `PMTK353`, `PMTK869` with 60 ms gaps — same pacing vritra_fc uses to avoid silent command drops

2. **`gnssSendCmd()`** (new helper): computes NMEA checksum and sends the framed command string to `gnssPort`

3. **`pollGnss()`**: changed `Serial2.available()` and `Serial2.read()` to `gnssPort->available()` and `gnssPort->read()` so it follows whatever port autodetect chose

4. **Second buffer `gnssBuf2[1024]`**: needed so Serial1 also gets a big buffer during the detection window

---

**How to read the boot log after flashing:** the first thing printed for GNSS will be one of:
- `Satellites  talking on Serial2 pins 7 and 8` — hardware is on the expected rev 2026-08 port
- `Satellites  talking on Serial1 pins 0 and 1` — hardware is still on the old port; vritra_fc was silently saving you, test_rf_tx was silently failing
- `Satellites  no data on either port after 3 seconds` — reset line or power problem

Flash this, check which line appears at boot, and type `STATUS` after a minute outdoors. Written and unverified.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:41:47)

Good news: the port autodetect worked — "talking on Serial2 pins 7 and 8" means the module IS sending data. The code changes are correct. This is now a hardware/location problem, not a firmware problem.

Looking at the photo — two things stand out:

**Issue 1 — the module is against a concrete wall.** The patch antenna on the L89HA faces straight up, but the wall beside it blocks roughly half the sky hemisphere. GNSS needs ~4 satellites visible above 15° elevation in different directions. With a wall that close, you might only see a narrow strip of sky and the module never acquires enough satellites.

**Issue 2 — the L89HA-S90 has an external antenna port.** I can see the u.FL connector on the right side of the 7SEMI breakout board. The "A" in L89HA often indicates the active-antenna variant. On that variant the external u.FL port is the primary antenna input — the module may not even use the ceramic patch as the main RF path. If that port has nothing connected, the receiver reports 0 sats regardless of sky view.

**Type STATUS right now.** You already have the code for it. It will print:
- `bytes seen` — should be nonzero (module is talking)
- `sentences read cleanly` — if this is 0, there's a parsing/checksum problem
- `last sentence heard` — if this is nonzero, paste that line here

**While waiting for STATUS, try one of these:**
1. Carry the board fully outside, flat on an open surface, nothing overhead or within 30° on any side. No building edge above you. 2–3 minutes cold-start time from that position.
2. If you have a small active GNSS patch antenna with a u.FL pigtail (the kind with a magnetic base), plug it into the connector on the right side of the 7SEMI board and lay it flat outdoors.

The code cannot fix a blocked sky or a missing antenna. Type STATUS and paste the three numbers — that will confirm which of the two it is.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:52:40)

There's already a `test_gnss` sketch. Let me check what's in it before deciding whether to rewrite or extend it.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:53:23)

The existing `test_gnss.ino` hardcodes Serial2, 9600 baud, and only parses GGA/GSA/RMC/GSV loosely. I'll rewrite it into a proper zero-assumption bring-up tool: raw byte view, port+baud scan, per-satellite signal table, and manual command control — so you can see exactly what the module is doing at every layer.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:55:02)

**Written and unverified** — no local toolchain, so this needs a real compile/flash to confirm it builds clean.

What this gives you that the old version didn't:

- **`SCAN`** — tries both Serial1 and Serial2 at 6 baud rates each (4800 → 115200), reports byte counts and whether a `$` was ever seen, then locks onto whichever combination actually answered. No more assuming pins 7/8 at 9600.
- **`RAW`** — toggles a live hex/text view of every byte the moment it lands, before any NMEA parsing touches it. Lets you tell "dead line" from "live line full of garbage" (e.g. inverted TX/RX, wrong baud, floating pin).
- **`SATS`** — full satellite-in-view table per constellation (GPS/GLONASS/Galileo/BeiDou/QZSS/NavIC), each with PRN, elevation, azimuth, and signal strength in dB-Hz, parsed from GSV. This is the key one: it answers "does the antenna see anything at all" completely independent of whether the solver has produced a fix. Zero here with the module talking correctly means sky/antenna, not code.
- **`STATUS`** — bytes seen, sentence pass/fail counts, fix type from GSA (not just GGA), DOP values, and an acknowledgement log so you can see every `$PAIR001`/`$PMTK001` response and confirm a command was actually accepted, not just sent.
- **`SEND <text>`** — fire any raw command manually with checksum computed for you, so you can probe the module interactively instead of only what's hardcoded.
- **`RESET`** — pulses pin 6 and restarts the cold-start timer on demand.

Flash it, open serial monitor, and the first thing to try is `SCAN` even before looking at anything else — that alone will tell you definitively whether the module is even reachable on the wiring as-is.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:59:22)

Two separate problems — one is a real bug in the code I wrote, the other is a board-selection issue.

**Bug 1 — `SysTable does not name a type`:** Arduino's IDE auto-generates function prototypes and inserts them at the very top of the file, before your own struct definitions appear. Since `sysFor()` returns `SysTable*`, the auto-generated prototype for it lands above where `SysTable` is defined, and the compiler chokes. Fix: eliminate that helper function and look the table up inline where it's used.

**Bug 2 — `addMemoryForRead` not found:** that call is Teensy-specific — it doesn't exist on generic `HardwareSerial`. This exact call worked in `test_rf_tx.ino` earlier, so this points at the board selected in Tools → Board not being **Teensy 4.1** for this sketch. Check that first; the code fix below won't help if the board target is wrong.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:59:26)

Now inline the lookup at the one call site in `parseGSV`:

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T07:59:37)

Fixed — `sysFor()` was the only function returning a custom struct pointer, so removing it (and inlining the lookup where it was actually used) resolves the auto-prototype ordering problem. No other function in the file returns or takes `SysTable`/`SatEntry` by value, so nothing else should trip the same issue.

Please also check **Tools → Board** is set to **Teensy 4.1** before recompiling — that's what the `addMemoryForRead` error is pointing at. Recompile and paste any remaining errors.

C:\Users\kalbh\AppData\Local\Temp\.arduinoIDE-unsaved202678-10708-55hxtp.2odlo\sketch_aug8a\sketch_aug8a.ino: In function 'void openPort(HardwareSerial*, uint32_t)':
C:\Users\kalbh\AppData\Local\Temp\.arduinoIDE-unsaved202678-10708-55hxtp.2odlo\sketch_aug8a\sketch_aug8a.ino:280:15: error: 'class HardwareSerial' has no member named 'addMemoryForRead'
  280 |     gnssPort->addMemoryForRead(port == &Serial2 ? rxBufA : rxBufB,
      |               ^~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
exit status 1

Compilation error: 'class HardwareSerial' has no member named 'addMemoryForRead'

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T08:00:15)

Good — the struct error is gone. This confirms it: the board selected in the IDE isn't Teensy 4.1. `addMemoryForRead()` is a Teensyduino-only extension to `HardwareSerial`; it doesn't exist on the generic Arduino core, ESP32 core, or any other board package. Since this exact call already worked when you flashed `test_rf_tx.ino` to the actual CanSat, the board dropdown has simply reverted or defaulted to something else for this sketch.

**Fix in the Arduino IDE:**
1. Tools → Board → find and select **Teensy 4.1** (under the Teensy boards section — you'll need the Teensyduino add-on installed if it's not there)
2. Tools → USB Type → should be **Serial** (or whatever you used successfully before)
3. Tools → Port → select the Teensy's COM port
4. Recompile

If Teensy doesn't appear in the Board menu at all, Teensyduino isn't installed in this Arduino IDE instance (possible if this is a different install/profile than the one you used for `test_rf_tx.ino`) — in that case let me know and I'll walk you through installing it.

**USER** (2026-08-08T08:03:49)

C:\Users\kalbh\Downloads\Cansat Ground station v2\cdr_tests\test_gnss\test_gnss.ino: In function 'void openPort(HardwareSerial*, uint32_t)':
C:\Users\kalbh\Downloads\Cansat Ground station v2\cdr_tests\test_gnss\test_gnss.ino:280:15: error: 'class HardwareSerial' has no member named 'addMemoryForRead'
  280 |     gnssPort->addMemoryForRead(port == &Serial2 ? rxBufA : rxBufB,
      |               ^~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
exit status 1

Compilation error: 'class HardwareSerial' has no member named 'addMemoryForRead' still use any other lib if required

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T08:04:09)

Found it. On Teensy 4.x, `addMemoryForRead()` is only a real method on the concrete `Serial1`/`Serial2` objects — it's not part of the abstract `HardwareSerial` base class those get passed around as. Since `gnssPort` is declared as a generic `HardwareSerial*` pointer, the compiler only sees the base class's interface, which doesn't include that method — even though the actual object underneath does have it. This is exactly why the original `test_rf_tx.ino` code (calling `Serial2.addMemoryForRead(...)` directly, not through a pointer) compiled fine.

Fix: call `addMemoryForRead()` directly on the named object, gated by which port we're opening, instead of through the pointer.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T08:04:19)

No other library needed — this was a base-class/derived-class visibility issue, not a missing dependency. Recompile and paste any further errors.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T08:09:51)

Good news — there's no firmware or wiring problem left. This run got a real fix:

**What happened:** After 128 seconds outdoors, the receiver locked a genuine 3D fix — 6 satellites, HDOP 1.4, PDOP 1.7, position holding to about 4.5 m of ordinary GPS jitter, altitude 253 m. The coordinates (31.2478°N, 75.7038°E) land right in Phagwara, Punjab — consistent with LPU's actual location. Bytes flowed from byte 1, checksums were 0 bad out of 1000+, every command you sent was acknowledged, and the raw NMEA dump confirms GPS + Galileo satellites tracked with real signal strengths (17–34 dB-Hz). The module, the wiring, the antenna, and this firmware are all doing exactly what they should.

**So what was the earlier "0 sats forever" problem?** Not a bug — it was an indoor/blocked-sky test. The 30-day trail of evidence (height near 0 m, degrading RSSI matching wall-test numbers, and now this outdoor run succeeding on the identical port/config code) all points the same way: satellites simply weren't visible from wherever the board was sitting before.

**The one real number left to fix: time to first fix was 128 s, not under 60 s.**

That's a genuine cold start — the receiver had no cached almanac or ephemeris for this location, so it had to search blind and download orbit data satellite by satellite, which is why the "satellites in view" count crept up 1 → 3 → 4 → 5 → 6 over two minutes instead of jumping straight there. This is exactly what `$PMTK869,1,1` (EASY) is for — it caches those orbit predictions to internal flash so the *next* power-up from roughly the same spot, within a few hours, starts warm and should fix in well under 60 seconds.

**Next step, no code changes needed:** without moving the board, type `RESET` (or power-cycle it) and time the fix again. If the warm start comes in under 60 s, TTFF is solved and there's nothing left to chase. If it's still slow on a warm start, that would point at weak signal (your SNRs topped out around 33–34 dB-Hz, which is fine but not strong — a clearer view of sky or a better antenna placement would help), not the code.

One cosmetic note, not a bug: `$PAIR010,0,0`/`$PAIR010,0,2` in your log got logged as "Acknowledged" — those are actually unsolicited status pushes the module sends on its own, not replies to a command you sent. Harmless, just a mislabel in the status printout if you want it cleaned up later.

**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T08:13:07)

Good catch, and you're right to push back — same board, same spot, same antenna, so it can't be a sky problem this time. The difference is real, and it's timing. Let me find it.

`test_gnss.ino` does nothing but drain the GNSS port every loop iteration. `test_rf_tx.ino` shares that same loop with the SD card and RTC. Comparing the two, `logRow()` is the only blocking I/O in the whole file:

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T08:13:23)

Found it. `logFile.sync()` (every 5th row, so every 5 seconds) is a card-level flush — on SDIO it can stall for hundreds of milliseconds to over a second on an ordinary microSD card doing internal housekeeping. `rtcReadStamp()` runs every single row too, an I2C transaction. Neither is huge on its own, but they're the *only* blocking calls sharing a loop with GNSS — and your 1024-byte buffer holds only about 1.07 seconds of GNSS traffic at 9600 baud. If `sync()` alone stalls anywhere near a second, which cheap cards routinely do, the buffer overflows mid-sentence, checksums fail, and `gnssFix` never completes — over and over, every 5-second cycle, forever. `test_gnss.ino` never touches an SD card, so it never hits this.

Two fixes: drain GNSS immediately on both sides of the slow SD call instead of only once per full loop pass, and give the buffer more headroom.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T08:13:32)

Now bracket `logRow()` with `pollGnss()` so bytes get drained right up to the moment the SD call starts, and again the instant it returns:

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-08-08T08:13:59)

**Written and unverified.** Three changes to `test_rf_tx.ino`:

1. **GNSS buffer `1024 → 3072` bytes** — was 1.07 s of headroom against a `sync()` that can stall for the better part of a second; now ~3.2 s, enough margin even for a bad sync.
2. **`pollGnss()` called on both sides of `logFile.sync()`** in `logRow()` — drains right up to the edge of the stall and again the instant it returns, instead of leaving the buffer to absorb the whole gap unaided.
3. **`pollGnss()` also called before the RTC's I2C read** in the same function, for the same reason, though that one was never the main suspect.

Nothing else in the file blocks — the radio transmit and listen window are both non-blocking state-machine calls, and `pollGnss()` already runs once per full `loop()` pass regardless. The SD sync every 5th row was the one call sharing the loop that `test_gnss.ino` never has to contend with, which is exactly why the standalone tool got a clean fix in the same spot this sketch couldn't.

Reflash `test_rf_tx.ino` at the same location and it should behave the same as your successful `test_gnss.ino` run — expect `Sats` to climb the same way (1 → 3 → 4 → 5 → 6) over the first 1-2 minutes instead of staying at 0.

`pytesseract` is just a Python wrapper — it needs the actual Tesseract OCR engine binary too. On Windows, the easiest path is winget:

```bash
winget install --id UB-Mannheim.TesseractOCR -e
```

For `qpdf` (command-line PDF merge/split/rotate, separate from the Python route):

```bash
winget install --id QPDF.QPDF -e
```

After any winget install, close and reopen your terminal so `PATH` picks up the new binary.
